 THIS WRIT PETITION (CIVIL) HAVING COME UP FOR ADMISSION
 ON 04.04.2022, ALONG WITH WP(C).10737/2022 AND CONNECTED
 CASES, THE COURT ON THE SAME DAY DELIVERED THE FOLLOWING:

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 415
 JUDGMENT
 These writ petitions have been filed by students pursuing their final
 year MBBS Course for the academic year 2017-2022 in various Medical
 Colleges in the State of Kerala. Their common grievance in these writ
 petitions is the decision taken by the Kerala University of Health and
 Sciences (KUHS) fixing the date for the III Professional MBBS Degree Part II
 Regular/Supplementary Examinations from 31.3.2022 onwards.
 2. As common issues are raised, all these writ petitions are taken
 up and disposed of by a common judgment. Reference to parties and
 exhibits shall be as described in W.P.(C) No.10737/2022 unless otherwise
 stated.
 3. The petitioners contend that the MBBS Course is split up into 9
 semesters consisting of three phases. Every student is expected to undergo
 a period of certified study extending over 4½ academic years divided into 9
 semesters of six months each from the date of commencement of her/his
 study for the subjects comprising the medical curriculum to the date of
 completion of examination followed by one year compulsory rotating
 internship. The final year falls in the third phase and it consists of

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 416
 semesters 8 and 9. As per Ext.P2 Regulations issued by the KUHS, the
 Colleges have to conduct a minimum of 44 weeks of classes for the 8th and
 9th semesters, out of which three hours a day is set apart for clinical
 posting, two hours for theory classes and the balance of two hours is set
 apart for practicals and seminars. In other words, the final year students
 have to undergo 738 hours of clinical postings, 492 hours of theory classes
 in clinical subjects and 492 hours for practical and seminars. Such a strict
 regimen is put in place to ensure that a student who is graduated becomes
 fully equipped to handle a human being who approaches the physician for
 his treatment. The Regulations mandate that only a candidate who has
 undergone the prescribed duration of study is eligible to qualify for a
 Degree.
 4. The petitioners assert that COVID-19 pandemic wrecked and
 disrupted the academic calendar. All the Colleges and the Universities had
 to remain closed to prevent the spread of the virus. None of the Colleges
 were able to impart the required mandatory training to the students,
 whether it be theory classes or clinical postings. Though the Regulations
 stipulate that 44 complete weeks of classes are to be held for the period
 from August 2021 to 16.2.2022, the Colleges were able to impart much less

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 417
 training. The overall shortage of training has been estimated by the
 petitioners as 16 weeks. The petitioners assert by relying on Exhibit P4 data
 that the deficiency of classes is more than 40% in some of the colleges. The
 petitioners contend that the impact of the deficiency is that none of the
 petitioners would be eligible to appear for the NEET PG Examination and
 even if they are graduate, they would only be half baked professionals.
 5. The petitioners state that taking note of the unprecedented
 situation, the Heads of Institutions of various Medical Colleges in the State
 had requested the Director of Medical Education (DME) to postpone the
 examinations. Several students had also approached the KUHS and the DME
 seeking postponement on the ground of lack of minimum training and when
 no action was taken, a group of students had approached this Court and
 had filed W.P.(C) No. 6038/2022. This Court, by judgment dated 14.3.2022,
 rejected the request for postponement of the examinations but issued
 certain directions based on the submissions made before this Court by the
 learned counsel appearing on both sides. It is contended that some of the
 Universities outside Kerala have scheduled the regular examinations of the
 Final Year MBBS Part I (2018-2019) and Part II (2017-2018) batches from
 the 3rd week of May 2022 and there is no reason why the KUHS cannot

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 418
 follow suit. It is on these grounds that these writ petitions have been filed
 seeking more or less the same relief. The reliefs prayed for are:
 i) To issue a writ of certiorari or any other appropriate writ,
 order or direction to quash Ext.P1 notification issued by the
 1st respondent University;
 ii) To issue a writ of mandamus or any other appropriate writ,
 order or direction to the 1st respondent University to
 reschedule the dates of III Professional MBBS Degree Part II
 Examination till the mandate under Ext.P2 regulations are
 met.
 6. In the statement filed by the University through their Standing
 Counsel on 28.3.2022, they admit that the medical courses were also
 affected due to the pandemic. Taking note of the said situation, the National
 Medical Commission issued Ext.R1A Schedule of Academic Program by
 which, the classes for the final year (III Professional Part II) had to be held
 from May 2021 to February, 2022 and the examinations were to be held in
 March 2022. The Medical Colleges in the State commenced online theory
 classes by the month of April 2021 and after commencement of the physical
 classes in August 2021, clinical and practical training were given priority and
 the loss occurred during the previous months were made good. As the

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 419
 student is required to obtain minimum prescribed attendance to attend the
 examination, the attendance and internal marks of each student has been
 uploaded by the college as part of the registration procedure for the
 examination. In terms of the notification issued by the KUHS, the colleges
 have uploaded the attendance report and the internal assessment marks to
 the web portal. A total of 2915 students had registered for the regular
 examination and 811 students had registered for the supplementary
 examination. The entire process for the conduct of the examination was
 completed by the University and hall tickets were also issued to the
 candidates. Many of the candidates which include the petitioners had
 registered for the examination. As both the regular and supplementary
 candidates have a common examination, the postponement of the
 examinations was out of the question as the students appearing for the
 supplementary examination cannot have a grievance that they were not
 properly trained. It is further stated that a large number of students are
 willing and eager to appear for the examinations conducted by the
 University and it would not be proper to postpone the examination to their
 detriment. Going by the assertions in the writ petition, the postponement
 of the examination is not the remedy. In order to provide the student with

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 420
 additional training, the course period has to be extended or otherwise, no
 college would come forward to impart training to the students, for which
 directions will have to be issued to the College to include the petitioners in
 additional batches and ensure that the students are satisfied with the clinical
 training. Though the KUHS had addressed the NMC for clarification, their
 request was not responded to. According to the respondents, the Post
 Graduate courses in the Medical stream are held on the basis of a time
 schedule and in the event of postponement of the MBBS Final Year
 Examination, many students who would otherwise be eligible to appear for
 the PG NEET Test will lose a chance if exams are postponed, as requested
 by the petitioners. Furthermore, the University schedules each examination
 well in advance as per a time table fixed by them and if any of the exams
 are adjourned, the entire time table would be disrupted. It is also stated
 that while disposing of W.P.(C) No.6038/2022, this Court, had issued orders
 directing the University to permit the petitioners therein to join the junior
 batch and the University was also directed to issue directions to the Colleges
 concerned to impart additional training. Those students who are similarly
 placed as the petitioners herein were permitted to appear for the III
 Professional MBBS Part II Examinations along with their junior batch in the

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 421
 exams which are tentatively scheduled to be held on 19.9.2022. It was
 further ordered that the said exam shall be treated as the regular
 examination for those students and not as a supplementary examination. It
 is stated that similar relief can be granted to the petitioners as well.
 7. In the course of proceedings, an application for impleadment
 has been filed by the parents of two students pursuing their Final Year
 MBBS Course. They have asserted in their affidavit that it would not be in
 the best interest of the willing students to postpone the examinations as it
 would impair their chances to appear for the NEET PG 2023. They have also
 stated that postponement of the course would result in a situation wherein
 they would be forced to pay additional tuition fee and hostel fee and the
 course will also get delayed.
 8. I have heard Sri.N.N.Sugunapalan, the learned senior counsel
 as instructed by Sri.S. Sujin, the learned counsel, Sri. P.B.Krishnan, the
 learned counsel appearing for some of the students, Sri. Biju Sundar, the
 learned counsel appearing for the petitioners in W.P.(C) No.12045/2022,
 Sri. P. Sreekumar, the learned standing counsel appearing for the KUHS, Sri
 P.C. Chacko and Smt. Surya Binoy, the learned Senior Government Pleader.

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 422
 9. A group of 37 students, similarly placed as the petitioners, had
 earlier approached this Court and by judgment dated 14.3.2022 in W.P.(C)
 No.6038/2022, this Court had refused to postpone the examinations
 scheduled from 31.3.2022 onwards. In the said writ petition, the petitioners
 had contended that the colleges in which they were pursuing their course
 had not imparted the necessary amount of training to enable the students to
 acquire the necessary knowledge and minimum level of skills. Their prayer
 was to issue directions to the KUHS to postpone the exams scheduled and
 to conduct the same only after completing the minimum requisite teaching
 hours prescribed for theory and clinical classes.
 10. This Court, while rejecting the request for postponement of
 exams, had disposed of the writ petition by issuing directions. It would be
 apposite to extract paragraph Nos.11 to 14 of the judgment.
 11. I find considerable merit in the submission of the learned
 standing counsel that the mere postponement of the exams will
 not augur to the cause of the petitioners as none of the colleges
 would impart any additional training as their course period is
 over. The petitioners will have to remain at home and prepare
 for the exams and that may not be the proper choice for them.
 12. In the statement filed by the respondents, they have

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 423
 stated that instead of postponement of the exams, a better
 course would be for the petitioners to join their junior batch and
 undergo training so that they can appear for the next exam
 which is scheduled to be held on 19.9.2022. The University has
 also offered to explore the possibility of treating the exams to be
 held on 19.9.2022 as their regular exam and not as their
 supplementary examination. This appears to be a better course
 as if the exams are postponed based on the contentions raised
 by the petitioners in this writ petition, it may adversely affect the
 other students and unsettle the entire process. The respondents
 are to ensure the availability of house surgeons in the hospitals
 and they are also to ensure that the present batch completes
 their intention before the joining time of the junior batches.
 13. Being conscious of the law laid down by the Apex Court in
 All India Council of Technical Education v. Surinder
 Kumar Dhawan [2009 (11) SCC 726] that courts should
 normally keep their hands off in issues involving academic
 matters, I am of the view that the prayer sought for by the
 petitioners seeking postponement of the exams cannot be
 allowed in the facts and circumstances.
 14. However, in view of the stand taken by the KUHS, the
 following directions are issued:
 a) The petitioners herein shall be permitted to join the junior
 batch and directions shall be given to the colleges concerned to
 grant additional training.

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 424
 b) The petitioners shall be permitted to appear for the Third
 Professional M.B.B.S Part II Examinations along with their junior
 batch in the exams which are tentatively scheduled to be held on
 19.9.2022. The said exam shall be treated as the regular
 examination and not as a supplementary examination insofar as
 the petitioners are concerned. It would be open to the University
 to extend the same benefit to other students who approach the
 University with similar grievances.
 c) Notwithstanding the above directions, after receipt of the
 attendance report from the colleges which is required to be
 uploaded on 18.3.2022, the University shall consider the
 grievances raised by the petitioners/other students
 sympathetically and issue directions to the concerned colleges to
 impart training to the students and compensate for the loss of
 training insofar as theory and clinical classes are concerned, if
 circumstances so warranted.
 d) In respect of those students who have already paid the
 examination fee but choose to appear for the examinations to be
 tentatively held on 19.9.2022, the fee already paid shall be
 adjusted for the next examination.
 11. These writ petitions were filed on 28.3.2022 and thereafter.
 When these writ petitions had come up for admission, this Court, after
 hearing the submissions of both sides, had passed an interim order on
 29.3.2022, the relevant portion reads as under:

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 425
 3. A statement has been filed by the learned Standing
 Counsel appearing for the KUHS. It is stated that a total of
 2915 students have registered for the regular examinations
 and 811 students have registered for the supplementary
 examinations. As the regular and supplementary candidates
 have a common examination, it would not be possible for the
 University to postpone the examinations which have already
 been scheduled. It is stated that it would be a difficult task to
 conduct another examination for the MBBS students of the 3rd
 professional Part II before September 2022 for logistic reasons.
 They have also stated that the University proposes to issue
 necessary orders in the light of the directions issued by this
 Court in W.P.(C) No.6038 of 2022 and that such a course
 would take care of the interest of all the students including
 those who are ready to appear for the examinations to be held
 on 31.03.2022.
 4. Sri. N.N. Sugunapalan, the learned Senior counsel
 appearing for the petitioners in these writ petitions as
 instructed by Sri. S.Sujin, the learned counsel appearing for the
 petitioners submitted that if the course proposed by the
 University is followed and the petitioners are permitted to write
 the regular examinations in the month of September 2022,
 they are likely to lose one year. It is further submitted by the
 learned Senior counsel that in the case of Kannur University,
 the Kerala University of Health Sciences (KUHS), taking note of
 the fact that the clinical postings have not been completed,
 had interdicted the University from conducting the

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 426
 examinations. The learned Senior counsel would submit that
 the situation can be saved, if the University takes a decision to
 prepone the examination from September 2022 to an earlier
 date so that the interest of the students can also be protected.
 It is also submitted that the Director of Medical Education had
 addressed all the colleges in the State of Kerala and all the
 Colleges had concurred to postpone the examinations.
 5. Sri. P. Sreekumar, the learned Standing Counsel
 appearing for the KUHS submitted that the University has
 considered all the relevant aspects and they have already
 taken a stand that the examinations can be held only in the
 month of September/October 2022 and not earlier.
 6. The learned Government Pleader also, on
 instructions, submitted that she has been instructed to submit
 that the postponement of the examinations as requested by
 the petitioners cannot be permitted.
 7. This Court has already considered the issue in
 W.P.(C) No. 6038/2022. From the submissions it appears that
 the petitioners are also not opposed to the steps proposed to
 be taken by the University which is in consonance with the
 directions issued by this Court earlier. The only request made
 by the learned Senior Counsel is to consider as to whether the
 exams can be preponed to an earlier date.
 The learned Standing Counsel appearing for the KUHS
 seeks an adjournment to reconfirm whether the exams can be

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 427
 preponed from September/October 2022 to an earlier date,
 under any circumstances.
 12. As is borne out from the order, from the submissions made
 before this Court, it was evident that the petitioners were also not opposed
 to the steps taken by the University in consonance with the directions issued
 by this Court in W.P.(C) No.6038/2022. Their only request was for
 preponement of the examinations to a date earlier than September 2022.
 The writ petition was accordingly posted on 30.3.2022, a day prior to the
 commencement of the exams.
 13. On 30.3.2022, when the matter was taken up, it was submitted
 before this Court by the learned Standing Counsel appearing for the
 University that the University shall assess the percentage of students, who
 would attend the exams on 31.3.2022 and thereafter the Board of
 Examinations would take a call as to whether there is any need to alter the
 examination time table that has been tentatively fixed for the academic
 year.
 14. Certain unfortunate events took place after the passing of the

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 428
 interim order. Some of the students as well as some lawyers posted
 messages in various social media networks that the exams are likely to be
 adjourned if the students, en masse boycott the exams. This message was
 circulated in the social media and most of the students got a wrong
 indication of the tenor and purport of the order passed by this Court. As a
 matter of fact, this Court was only exploring the possibilities to ensure that
 the interests of the students are protected without disrupting the academic
 calendar.
 15. An additional statement has been filed by the learned Standing
 Counsel for the KUHS on 4.4.2022 to place on record the events that had
 transpired after the interim order dated 30.3.2022. It is stated that certain
 persons circulated posts in the social media to the effect that an additional
 examination would be conducted if the students boycott examinations to be
 held on 31/03/2022. A screenshot of the post is produced as Ext.R1D. Out
 of 3687 registered candidates, 1516 candidates appeared for the
 examination and 2171 candidates remained absent. In order to prevent the
 spread of misinformation, the University convened an urgent meeting of the
 Principals of the Medical Colleges on 31/03/2022. The Principals have
 informed the University that classes were provided to the students and the

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 429
 assertions to the contrary are not correct. It was the unanimous opinion of
 the Heads of institutions that it would not be possible for advancing the
 scheduled examination as the training of additional batches have been
 arranged in such a way that they could be presented for the examination in
 the month of September. Ext.R1E is the minutes of the meeting which has
 been placed on record.
 16. As stated earlier, being conscious of the earlier directions issued
 by this Court in W.P. (C) No. 6038 of 2022, the counsel appearing for the
 petitioners did not insist that the exams be postponed. They have more or
 less accepted the fact that hundreds of students are eager and willing to
 appear for the examination. Their only complaint is that the University is
 taking an inflexible stand without considering the plight of the students.
 17. Sri.N.N.Sugunapalan, the learned Senior Counsel and
 Sri.P.B.Krishnan, the learned counsel, took pains to explain that the conduct
 of the exams in the month of September 2022 would be detrimental to the
 interests of the students as it would prevent them from appearing for the
 NEET PG exams. However, I find from the writ petition that it is the specific
 case of the petitioners that the shortage of training is to the extent of 16

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 430
 weeks. If the course period is extended as suggested by the petitioners, it
 would take time until August end for completing the training. If that be the
 case, the exams can be held only in the month of September, 2022 as
 scheduled.
 18. However, I note that a large number of students have, for one
 reason or the other, failed to attend the exams. While disposing of the
 earlier writ petition, this Court, considering the interest of the students, had
 ordered the respondents to extend the same benefit to other similarly
 placed students who approach the University with the same grievance. That
 may be one of the reasons why they chose to skip the exams so that they
 can appear for the September 2022 exams as their regular first chance.
 They also may have been persuaded by their fellow students or advisors to
 boycott the exams under the belief that the University would change their
 stand and conduct the exams earlier. Be that as it may, I am of the
 considered opinion that postponement of the exams as prayed for by the
 petitioners cannot be granted. That was the view taken by this Court earlier
 and there is no reason to take a different view now. However, that does not
 mean that the University shall not consider the cause of the students
 sympathetically as the University is duty bound to protect the interest and

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 431
 welfare of the students. Notwithstanding the rejection of the prayer for
 adjournment of the exams, the University shall reconsider their grievances
 sympathetically after getting fresh inputs from the Principals of the Colleges
 concerned. I record the submission of the learned Standing Counsel that the
 University shall place the matter before the Board of Examinations which
 shall meet within a month and a decision shall be taken as regards the
 conduct of the exams for the students who did not attend the exams,
 whatever be the reason. The Board of Examinations shall take a decision on
 the grievances of the students with regard to preponement of the regular
 exams scheduled to be held during September, 2022 within a month from
 today after evaluating the entire aspects.
 19. Before parting, I take note of the submissions of Sri P.C.
 Chacko, that efforts are being made by a group of students to spread false
 information and to threaten the students who are willing to appear for the
 exams. No such attempts can be tolerated. This Court at no point of time
 had given an indication that the Examinations scheduled would be
 interdicted or postponed. The examinations as scheduled shall go on and
 the University as well as the Colleges shall ensure that all the willing
 students are able to appear for the examinations without fear.

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 432
 In view of the discussion above, I am of the considered opinion that
 the petitioners have not made any case for quashing Ext.P1 notification or
 for rescheduling the dates of the III Professional MBBS Degree Part II
 Examinations. The students who failed to appear for the III Professional
 MBBS Degree Part II Examinations shall be permitted to appear for the
 Examinations along with their junior batch in the exams which are
 tentatively scheduled to be held on 19.9.2022, or such other date as
 modified by the Board of Examinations pursuant to the exercise as ordered
 by this Court. Necessary directions shall be issued to the Colleges to permit
 the petitioners to join the junior batch or to constitute additional batches so
 that the petitioners may obtain training as sought for by them. The
 University shall take immediate steps to convene the Board of
 Examinations/appropriate body of the University, which body shall consider
 the grievance of the students and also their request for preponing the
 exams from 19.9.2022 as tentatively fixed to an earlier date. While
 considering the request, the Board of Examinations shall take necessary
 inputs from the Head of institutions of various Colleges and such other
 persons that they deem fit and proper. The Board of
 Examinations/appropriate body of University shall take a decision as directed

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 433
 above within a period of one month from the date of receipt of a copy of
 this judgment.
 These writ petitions are disposed of.
 Sd/-
 RAJA VIJAYARAGHAVAN V,
 JUDGE
 DSV

 WP(C) NO. 10737 OF 2022 &
 CONN. CASES
 434

 FORWARDED BY PRINCIPAL, GOVERNMENT MEDICAL
 COLLEGE, KOTTAYAM.

 2022 DATED 14.3.2022.
 Exhibit P11 TRUE COPY OF THE STATEMENT DATED 3.3.2022
 FILED BY THE 1ST RESPONDENT IN WP(C) NO.6038
